# Rice protein

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="rice-protein-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="rice-protein-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="rice-protein-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Rice protein carries inorganic arsenic and cadmium from the rice grain.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Canadian Food Inspection Agency · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Toxic Metals in Selected Foods – April 1, 2018 to March 31, 2019: Food chemistry – Targeted surveys – Final report</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Food Inspection Agency</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Lead has no quantified result, and no result is linked to where the rice was grown.

<ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals of concern:</strong>

Inorganic arsenic was quantified in 17 of 19 US samples at 25 to 152 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Cadmium and mercury have one study and one sample each.<sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Canadian Food Inspection Agency · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Toxic Metals in Selected Foods – April 1, 2018 to March 31, 2019: Food chemistry – Targeted surveys – Final report</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Food Inspection Agency</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Arsenic concentrates in rice bran, which brown rice keeps.<sup class="cite">[[sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Scott CK, Wu F · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Arsenic content and exposure in brown rice compared to white rice in the United States</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Risk Analysis</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Powders labeled brown rice protein measured higher inorganic arsenic than those labeled rice protein in a small comparison.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for inorganic arsenic, not total arsenic only, and cadmium on every lot. Summary arsenic figures can include substituted values.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
</ul>
</section>


<a id="decision-summary"></a>
## Rice protein overview

<a id="hmi-ing-004"></a>
<a id="which-contamination-concerns-are-supported-for-this-ingredient-and-its-defined-forms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-005"></a>
<a id="which-sourcing-or-production-decisions-deserve-priority-on-the-available-evidence"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-006"></a>
<a id="which-evidence-gaps-most-limit-those-decisions"></a>

Inorganic arsenic is the best-documented metal in rice protein: FDA sampled 19 rice protein powders from US retail in its 2012 to 2013 rice survey, and 17 had quantified inorganic arsenic between 25 and 152 µg/kg, with a median of 47 µg/kg (an Index calculation).<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The other two had total arsenic below the 10 µg/kg detection limit.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Cadmium is the second concern: five batches of rice endosperm protein made in Japan averaged 0.14 mg/kg with a standard deviation of 0.17 mg/kg, and a Canadian survey reported that rice-based protein powders had the highest metal levels of the protein types it tested, especially cadmium.<sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Canadian Food Inspection Agency · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Toxic Metals in Selected Foods – April 1, 2018 to March 31, 2019: Food chemistry – Targeted surveys – Final report</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Food Inspection Agency</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> One brown rice protein powder in that survey had 0.022 ppm mercury, the survey maximum.<sup class="cite">[[sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Canadian Food Inspection Agency · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Toxic Metals in Selected Foods – April 1, 2018 to March 31, 2019: Food chemistry – Targeted surveys – Final report</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Food Inspection Agency</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Lead has no quantified rice protein result, and no result is linked to where the rice was grown, so China, Vietnam, the USA and Canada cannot be compared as origins.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For a buyer, that makes lot testing for inorganic arsenic and cadmium the decision point rather than origin.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as rice protein

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

This page covers protein isolated or concentrated from rice, including brown rice protein, sold as powder or used as an ingredient. FDA classified 12 samples as rice protein powders and included 7 more rice protein products under protein beverages, measured as powder before mixing.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The cadmium study used rice endosperm protein made from milled rice flour, which is a different fraction from brown rice protein.<sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

FDA's rice protein powder category includes blends and meal replacements as well as single-protein powders, so those 12 samples describe the category as FDA sampled it.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Rice grain, bran, flour and drinks are on [[ingredients/rice]], pea protein on [[ingredients/pea-protein]], and mixed formulas on the protein powder product page.

<a id="hmi-ing-008"></a>
<a id="what-concentrations-detection-frequencies-and-variability-were-observed-in-each-defined-sampling-frame"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-009"></a>
<a id="which-analyte-questions-are-unassessed-access-blocked-conflicting-or-searched-without-eligible-evidence"></a>

The table lists every rice protein result in the Index: 84 rows from four studies.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span methylated arsenic species for up to 19 samples, measured in the powder as sold.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results protein samples; they are not market-wide grades or safety grades.

Total arsenic in the 17 FDA samples with a reported value, three of them trace results, ran from 60 to 260 µg/kg, median 100 µg/kg, and inorganic arsenic from 25 to 152 µg/kg, median 47 µg/kg (Index calculations).<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · <div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-rice-protein" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-rice-protein-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of rice protein, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Each source drawer identifies methods and overlapping samples. No matched reference or pooled distribution is inferred.</p></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:rice-protein-8db3b75ddfe7:4aa66a133211"><summary>HMI rice protein tAs reference · Rice protein, powder or ground</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:rice-protein-8db3b75ddfe7:4aa66a133211"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI rice protein tAs reference · Rice protein, powder or ground. Rice protein, powder or ground; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>86</td><td>100</td><td>120</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>97</td><td>245</td><td>260</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>13 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>U.S. Food and Drug Administration 2013</td><td>13</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for rice protein, powder or ground on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>U.S. Food and Drug Administration 2013 is the only compatible sampling family for rice protein, powder or ground on this basis in the completed rice protein ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling" data-no-cite-meta>U.S. Food and Drug Administration 2013</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-11-02. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:rice-protein-8db3b75ddfe7:4aa66a133211</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:rice-protein-203badecdc99:8183dacc2f23"><summary>HMI rice protein tAs reference · Rice protein, as sold</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:rice-protein-203badecdc99:8183dacc2f23"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI rice protein tAs reference · Rice protein, as sold. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>U.S. Food and Drug Administration 2013 is the only compatible sampling family for rice protein, as sold on this basis in the completed rice protein ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. No cross-study trend is calculated in this comparison mode.</p><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reported values, conversion and source locator</summary><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Source statistic</th><th scope="col">Original result</th><th scope="col">Displayed unit conversion</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>not_detected</td><td>ND</td><td>as recorded in the findings store</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>Table 3, p. 5.</p><p>No moisture, cooking-yield or species conversion is applied to the original finding above. Any basis conversion used for its HMI comparison is stated separately with the numerical target.</p></details><p><a href="https://doi.org/10.1017/jns.2025.10024">Open the primary publication</a> · <a href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals" class="internal" data-slug="sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">HMI source record</a></p></div></div></div><p class="hmi-explorer-empty" data-explorer-empty hidden>No findings match these filters. Change the selection or reset to show all source findings.</p><dialog class="hmi-evidence-drawer" id="hmi-occurrence-rice-protein-drawer" aria-labelledby="hmi-occurrence-rice-protein-drawer-title" aria-modal="true"><header class="hmi-drawer-header"><div><p class="hmi-drawer-title" id="hmi-occurrence-rice-protein-drawer-title" role="heading" aria-level="2">1 dataset</p><p data-drawer-subtitle></p></div><button type="button" data-close-evidence aria-label="Close dataset details">×</button></header><div class="hmi-drawer-body" data-drawer-body></div></dialog></div>

<a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach rice protein

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

Rice is grown in flooded paddies, and the rice plant takes up arsenic and cadmium from paddy soil and water; [[ingredients/rice]] reviews that evidence in detail. Within the grain, arsenic concentrates in the outer bran and germ layers, which brown rice keeps and white rice loses in milling.<sup class="cite">[[sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Scott CK, Wu F · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Arsenic content and exposure in brown rice compared to white rice in the United States</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Risk Analysis</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

No study in the Index traces metals from paddy to rice protein, or measures contamination added during extraction, drying or packaging.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The Canadian survey links higher cadmium in protein powders to rice-based products but does not identify where in the supply chain it enters.<sup class="cite">[[sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Canadian Food Inspection Agency · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Toxic Metals in Selected Foods – April 1, 2018 to March 31, 2019: Food chemistry – Targeted surveys – Final report</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Food Inspection Agency</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>


<a id="concentration-ranges-by-country-of-origin"></a>
<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which rice protein sources measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-distinguishes-within-lot-between-lot-supplier-and-production-system-variation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

The clearest lower-arsenic signal is by product label within the FDA survey: three powders labeled rice protein had 25, 30 and 47 µg/kg inorganic arsenic, against 37, 53 and 99 µg/kg in three powders labeled brown rice protein, a fourth of which was below detection.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> That direction fits the higher arsenic of bran-containing brown rice, but with three or four samples per group it is a tentative difference, not a supported preference.<sup class="cite">[[sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Scott CK, Wu F · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Arsenic content and exposure in brown rice compared to white rice in the United States</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Risk Analysis</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

By FDA category, the six quantified protein beverage powders had a median inorganic arsenic of 42 µg/kg and the eleven quantified rice protein powder category samples a median of 50 µg/kg; the highest sample, 152 µg/kg, was a blend of protein, fruits and vegetables (Index calculations).<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The categories overlap widely, so they do not separate lower and higher sources.

No rice protein result is linked to the country where the rice was grown: FDA product rows carry no origin, the Canadian mercury sample gives none, and the endosperm protein was made in Japan from Koshihikari rice flour whose growing location is not stated.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Canadian Food Inspection Agency · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Toxic Metals in Selected Foods – April 1, 2018 to March 31, 2019: Food chemistry – Targeted surveys – Final report</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Food Inspection Agency</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> A brown rice protein powder produced in the USA was below an unprinted detection limit for all four metals, which cannot rank it.<sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Comparing China, Vietnam, the USA and Canada needs lot results for rice protein linked to the growing region.

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="is-brown-rice-protein-higher-in-arsenic-than-other-rice-protein" data-anchor="is-brown-rice-protein-higher-in-arsenic-than-other-rice-protein" data-question="Is brown rice protein higher in arsenic than other rice protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Is brown rice protein higher in arsenic than other rice protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

In the FDA survey, powders labeled brown rice protein had 37 to 99 µg/kg inorganic arsenic where quantified, and powders labeled rice protein had 25 to 47 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Rice bran concentrates arsenic, which fits that direction.<sup class="cite">[[sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Scott CK, Wu F · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Arsenic content and exposure in brown rice compared to white rice in the United States</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Risk Analysis</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Three or four samples per group make it tentative.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="can-country-of-origin-pick-a-lower-arsenic-rice-protein" data-anchor="can-country-of-origin-pick-a-lower-arsenic-rice-protein" data-question="Can country of origin pick a lower-arsenic rice protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Can country of origin pick a lower-arsenic rice protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Not on current evidence: no rice protein result names the growing region.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Canadian Food Inspection Agency · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Toxic Metals in Selected Foods – April 1, 2018 to March 31, 2019: Food chemistry – Targeted surveys – Final report</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Food Inspection Agency</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The rice grain evidence on origin does not transfer to protein without measurements of the protein itself.

</div>
</details>


<a id="processing-effects"></a>
<a id="derivatives-and-concentrates"></a>
## Processing effects and derivatives

<a id="hmi-ing-016"></a>
<a id="how-do-specified-processing-or-preparation-steps-change-the-measured-metal-endpoints"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-017"></a>
<a id="how-do-derivatives-concentrates-extracts-and-fractions-differ-from-their-parent-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-018"></a>
<a id="do-changes-reflect-removal-redistribution-moisture-or-yield-change-dilution-or-altered-species-or-availability"></a>

The endosperm protein study washed its protein four times with dilute hydrochloric acid to remove minerals and heavy metals such as cadmium, but it reports no cadmium value before washing, so how much the step removed is unmeasured.<sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Milling removes the bran and germ where arsenic concentrates, so protein made from milled rice starts from a lower-arsenic fraction than brown rice protein.<sup class="cite">[[sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Scott CK, Wu F · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Arsenic content and exposure in brown rice compared to white rice in the United States</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Risk Analysis</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> No study compares arsenic in rice protein made from brown rice with protein made from milled rice; the FDA label comparison is the nearest evidence.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>



<a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for rice protein

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

In a pot experiment, spraying rice with silicon lowered cadmium in brown rice grain by 13.5 to 65.7 percent, depending on variety.<sup class="cite">[[sources/zhao2023-silicon-cd-rice|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Zhao Z, Wei X, Zhao H, Liu M, Zhao Y, Ding Y · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/zhao2023-silicon-cd-rice">Exogenous Silicon Alleviates Cadmium Stress by Promoting Phenylpropanoid Metabolism in Rice</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Plant Science</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/zhao2023-silicon-cd-rice">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> That is a grain result; its effect on rice protein has not been measured.

Acid washing during protein extraction is described as a cadmium removal step without a measured result.<sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For a buyer, the verifiable control is lot testing of the finished protein for inorganic arsenic and cadmium with stated quantification limits.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Controls that lower arsenic in rice can raise cadmium: in a two-year field study, the driest water management lowered grain arsenic and mercury but raised grain cadmium by 50 to 97 percent against the most flooded paddy, and cut yield by 25 to 40 percent.<sup class="cite">[[sources/seyfferth2025-rice-water-management-as-hg-cd|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Seyfferth AL, Limmer MA, Jackson BP, Runkle BRK · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/seyfferth2025-rice-water-management-as-hg-cd">Concentrations and Health Implications of As, Hg, and Cd and Micronutrients in Rice and Emissions of CH4 From Variably Flooded Paddies</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GeoHealth</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/seyfferth2025-rice-water-management-as-hg-cd">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> A rice protein supplier changing its rice source or growing practice therefore needs both metals retested.

The FDA data also show that total arsenic alone can mislead: across the 17 samples with both values, inorganic arsenic was between 32 and 72 percent of total arsenic (Index calculation).<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> A specification on total arsenic does not fix the inorganic fraction.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing rice protein for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

FDA measured total arsenic and its species in the powder, with a 10 µg/kg detection limit and 80 µg/kg quantification limit for total arsenic and 2 and 12 µg/kg for inorganic arsenic in this product group.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Where total arsenic was below the speciation threshold, FDA did not speciate and used half the total arsenic value as the inorganic arsenic value.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A brown rice protein result reported as below detection without a printed limit shows nothing about how low the metals were.<sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> A useful certificate names the accredited laboratory, the method, the quantification limit for each metal, inorganic arsenic by speciation and the lot tested.



<a id="procurement-and-process-control"></a>
## Buying and verifying rice protein

<a id="hmi-ing-028"></a>
<a id="what-origin-traceability-processing-and-analytical-information-should-a-buyer-request"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-029"></a>
<a id="which-control-or-specification-choices-are-supported-and-which-are-policy-choices-requiring-separate-justification"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-030"></a>
<a id="what-supplier-process-or-test-result-changes-should-trigger-investigation-or-requalification"></a>

A buyer should ask for the testing laboratory's name and ISO/IEC 17025 accreditation, the report number for each certificate, the quantification limit for each metal, inorganic arsenic by speciation rather than total arsenic alone, and results for the lot being bought. These answer the gaps in the published evidence: unquantified lead, substituted arsenic values and missing detection limits.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Ask whether the protein is made from brown rice or milled rice, because the label comparison points to more inorganic arsenic in brown rice protein.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For a product sold in California at a 30 g serving, the lead concentration that reaches the 0.5 µg/day level is 16.7 µg/kg, so a lead quantification limit well below that is needed.<sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing|8]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment · Not reported</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Lead and Lead Compounds — Proposition 65 Chemical Listing</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Proposition 65 Listings</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Choosing a specification at such a figure is a policy decision for the buyer, not a value the evidence sets.



<a id="finished-product-and-exposure-implications"></a>
## Exposure from rice protein

<a id="hmi-ing-031"></a>
<a id="how-do-inclusion-rate-processing-yield-preparation-and-other-ingredients-affect-a-finished-product-estimate"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-032"></a>
<a id="how-do-consumption-patterns-and-intended-populations-affect-dietary-exposure-interpretation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-033"></a>
<a id="what-conclusions-require-finished-product-measurements-or-a-separate-exposure-assessment"></a>

FDA calculated inorganic arsenic per serving using each product's reference serving, 18 to 85 g for the rice protein powder category; the category averaged 1.9 µg per serving, ranging from 0.1 to 5.7 µg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> At a 30 g scoop, the median inorganic arsenic of 47 µg/kg gives 1.41 µg, an Index calculation; the Index does not yet hold California's inorganic arsenic level.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-arsenic-prop65-listing|9]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment · Not reported</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-arsenic-prop65-listing">Arsenic (inorganic Arsenic Compounds) — Proposition 65 Chemical Listing</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Proposition 65 Listings</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-arsenic-prop65-listing">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For cadmium, the endosperm protein mean of 0.14 mg/kg gives 4.2 µg in a 30 g scoop, against California's oral cadmium level of 4.1 µg/day.<sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-cadmium-prop65-madl-2001|10]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Reproductive and Cancer Hazard Assessment Section · 2001</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-cadmium-prop65-madl-2001">Proposition 65 Maximum Allowable Daily Level (MADL) for Reproductive Toxicity for Cadmium (Oral Route)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Environmental Protection Agency, Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-cadmium-prop65-madl-2001">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> That is a mean of five batches with a wide spread, for endosperm protein rather than brown rice protein, and whether a product needs a warning depends on its labeled serving, daily use and the law's other provisions.

## Requirements and evidence review

<a id="hmi-ing-034"></a>
<a id="which-primary-regulatory-instruments-apply-to-this-exact-ingredient-form-species-market-and-date"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-035"></a>
<a id="what-searches-eligibility-decisions-source-families-and-appraisal-underpin-the-answers-and-certainty-judgments"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-036"></a>
<a id="what-remains-unresolved-when-was-each-answer-reviewed-and-what-changed-in-this-version"></a>

The EU sets cadmium at 0.15 mg/kg for rice and inorganic arsenic at 0.15 mg/kg for non-parboiled milled rice and 0.25 mg/kg for parboiled rice, husked rice and rice flour; none of these rows names rice protein.<sup class="cite">[[sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels|11]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Commission · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Commission Regulation (EU) 2023/915 of 25 April 2023 on maximum levels for certain contaminants in food and repealing Regulation (EC) No 1881/2006</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Official Journal of the European Union</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> California sets lead at 0.5 µg/day and oral cadmium at 4.1 µg/day.<sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing|8]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment · Not reported</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Lead and Lead Compounds — Proposition 65 Chemical Listing</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span arsenic action level of 100 µg/kg applies to infant rice cereal, not rice protein.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2020-ias-infant-rice-cereal-guidance|12]]<span class="cite-card"><span replaced it from 2026-09-02.<sup class="cite">[[sources/chinanhc2025-gb-2762-contaminants|14]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">National Health
