References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Rice protein FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-arsenic-is-in-rice-protein" data-anchor="how-much-arsenic-is-in-rice-protein" data-question="How much arsenic is in rice protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much arsenic is in rice protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

In FDA's US retail sampling, 17 of 19 rice protein samples had quantified inorganic arsenic from 25 to 152 µg/kg, median 47 µg/kg (Index calculation).<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> These samples date from 2012 to 2013.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="why-does-rice-protein-contain-arsenic" data-anchor="why-does-rice-protein-contain-arsenic" data-question="Why does rice protein contain arsenic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Why does rice protein contain arsenic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The rice plant takes up arsenic from flooded paddy soil, and arsenic concentrates in the bran and germ that brown rice keeps.<sup class="cite">[[sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Scott CK, Wu F · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Arsenic content and exposure in brown rice compared to white rice in the United States</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Risk Analysis</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/scott2025-brown-white-rice-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Rice protein is made from that grain.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="is-inorganic-arsenic-or-total-arsenic-the-right-test-for-rice-protein" data-anchor="is-inorganic-arsenic-or-total-arsenic-the-right-test-for-rice-protein" data-question="Is inorganic arsenic or total arsenic the right test for rice protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Is inorganic arsenic or total arsenic the right test for rice protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic: in FDA's samples it was between 32 and 72 percent of total arsenic, so a total figure does not give the inorganic share.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Rice limits are set for inorganic arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels|11]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Commission · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Commission Regulation (EU) 2023/915 of 25 April 2023 on maximum levels for certain contaminants in food and repealing Regulation (EC) No 1881/2006</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Official Journal of the European Union</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-rice-protein-contain-cadmium" data-anchor="does-rice-protein-contain-cadmium" data-question="Does rice protein contain cadmium?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does rice protein contain cadmium?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Rice endosperm protein averaged 0.14 mg/kg cadmium across five batches, and a Canadian survey found rice-based protein powders highest in cadmium among the protein types it tested.<sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Canadian Food Inspection Agency · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Toxic Metals in Selected Foods – April 1, 2018 to March 31, 2019: Food chemistry – Targeted surveys – Final report</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Food Inspection Agency</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> No quantified result exists for brown rice protein.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-rice-protein-contain-lead" data-anchor="does-rice-protein-contain-lead" data-question="Does rice protein contain lead?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does rice protein contain lead?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

No quantified result exists; one brown rice protein powder was below an unprinted detection limit.<sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Ask for lead with a stated quantification limit.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-rice-protein-contain-mercury" data-anchor="does-rice-protein-contain-mercury" data-question="Does rice protein contain mercury?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does rice protein contain mercury?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

One brown rice protein powder in a Canadian survey had 0.022 ppm mercury, the highest value in that survey.<sup class="cite">[[sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Canadian Food Inspection Agency · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Toxic Metals in Selected Foods – April 1, 2018 to March 31, 2019: Food chemistry – Targeted surveys – Final report</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Food Inspection Agency</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="is-brown-rice-protein-higher-in-arsenic-than-other-rice-protein" data-anchor="is-brown-rice-protein-higher-in-arsenic-than-other-rice-protein" data-question="Is brown rice protein higher in arsenic than other rice protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Is brown rice protein higher in arsenic than other rice protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

In a small FDA comparison, yes: 37 to 99 µg/kg inorganic arsenic in brown rice protein against 25 to 47 µg/kg in rice protein where quantified.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The comparison is tentative.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="is-rice-protein-higher-in-arsenic-than-pea-protein" data-anchor="is-rice-protein-higher-in-arsenic-than-pea-protein" data-question="Is rice protein higher in arsenic than pea protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Is rice protein higher in arsenic than pea protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Pea protein has no arsenic data, so they cannot be compared. Rice protein's inorganic arsenic is documented in 17 samples.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> See [[ingredients/pea-protein]].

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-country-grows-the-lowest-arsenic-rice-for-protein" data-anchor="which-country-grows-the-lowest-arsenic-rice-for-protein" data-question="Which country grows the lowest-arsenic rice for protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which country grows the lowest-arsenic rice for protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

No rice protein result names the growing region, so China, Vietnam, the USA and Canada cannot be compared for rice protein.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Canadian Food Inspection Agency · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Toxic Metals in Selected Foods – April 1, 2018 to March 31, 2019: Food chemistry – Targeted surveys – Final report</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Food Inspection Agency</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfia2020-toxic-metals-selected-foods-2018-19">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Rice grain origin data are on [[ingredients/rice]] but do not convert directly to protein.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="can-processing-remove-arsenic-or-cadmium-from-rice-protein" data-anchor="can-processing-remove-arsenic-or-cadmium-from-rice-protein" data-question="Can processing remove arsenic or cadmium from rice protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Can processing remove arsenic or cadmium from rice protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Acid washing is used to remove cadmium during extraction, but its effect was not measured.<sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> No arsenic removal step for rice protein has been measured.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-do-i-check-a-rice-protein-against-california-prop-65" data-anchor="how-do-i-check-a-rice-protein-against-california-prop-65" data-question="How do I check a rice protein against California Prop 65?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How do I check a rice protein against California Prop 65?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Multiply concentration by serving weight: 0.14 mg/kg cadmium × 30 g = 4.2 µg, against the 4.1 µg/day cadmium level.<sup class="cite">[[sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Hosojima M, Shimada H, Obi Y, Kuwahara S, Kaseda R, Kabasawa H, Kondo H, Fujii M, Watanabe R, Suzuki Y, Kadowaki M, Miyazaki S, Saito A · 2017</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">A Randomized, Double-Blind, Crossover Pilot Trial of Rice Endosperm Protein Supplementation in Maintenance Hemodialysis Patients</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientific Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/hosojima2017-rice-endosperm-protein-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-cadmium-prop65-madl-2001|10]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Reproductive and Cancer Hazard Assessment Section · 2001</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-cadmium-prop65-madl-2001">Proposition 65 Maximum Allowable Daily Level (MADL) for Reproductive Toxicity for Cadmium (Oral Route)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Environmental Protection Agency, Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-cadmium-prop65-madl-2001">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The Index does not yet hold the inorganic arsenic level.<sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-arsenic-prop65-listing|9]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment · Not reported</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-arsenic-prop65-listing">Arsenic (inorganic Arsenic Compounds) — Proposition 65 Chemical Listing</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Proposition 65 Listings</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-arsenic-prop65-listing">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-does-not-detected-mean-on-a-rice-protein-certificate" data-anchor="what-does-not-detected-mean-on-a-rice-protein-certificate" data-question="What does &quot;not detected&quot; mean on a rice protein certificate?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What does &quot;not detected&quot; mean on a rice protein certificate?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

It means below the laboratory limit; one survey reported a brown rice protein as below detection without printing the limit.<sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> FDA replaced non-speciated results with half the total arsenic value, which is a substitute, not a measurement.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-should-a-rice-protein-suppliers-test-report-include" data-anchor="what-should-a-rice-protein-suppliers-test-report-include" data-question="What should a rice protein supplier&#39;s test report include?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What should a rice protein supplier&#39;s test report include?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The accredited laboratory's name and ISO/IEC 17025 status, the report number, the method, quantification limits, inorganic arsenic by speciation, cadmium, lead and mercury, the rice type and the lot tested. The published evidence lacks quantified lead and growing origin.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Analytical Results from Inorganic Arsenic in Rice and Rice Products Sampling, September 2013</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2013-ias-rice-products-sampling">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-the-eu-limit-metals-in-rice-protein" data-anchor="does-the-eu-limit-metals-in-rice-protein" data-question="Does the EU limit metals in rice protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does the EU limit metals in rice protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

No row names rice protein; rice rows set cadmium at 0.15 mg/kg and inorganic arsenic at 0.15 or 0.25 mg/kg depending on the rice form.<sup class="cite">[[sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels|11]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Commission · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Commission Regulation (EU) 2023/915 of 25 April 2023 on maximum levels for certain contaminants in food and repealing Regulation (EC) No 1881/2006</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Official Journal of the European Union</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-china-set-a-limit-for-rice-protein" data-anchor="does-china-set-a-limit-for-rice-protein" data-question="Does China set a limit for rice protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does China set a limit for rice protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

GB 2762-2022 has no protein powder row; rice powder is limited to 0.2 mg/kg cadmium and 0.2 mg/kg inorganic arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/usda-fas2023-china-gb-2762-2022-translation|13]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">USDA Foreign Agricultural Service, FAS China Staff (Beijing) · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/usda-fas2023-china-gb-2762-2022-translation">China Releases the Standard for Maximum Levels of Contaminants in Foods (USDA FAS GAIN Report CH2023-0040, unofficial translation of GB 2762-2022)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">USDA Foreign Agricultural Service, Global Agricultural Information Network (GAIN), Report Number CH2023-0040</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/usda-fas2023-china-gb-2762-2022-translation">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The 2025 edition replaced it from 2026-09-02.<sup class="cite">[[sources/chinanhc2025-gb-2762-contaminants|14]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">National Health Commission, State Administration for Market Regulation · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/chinanhc2025-gb-2762-contaminants">GB 2762-2025: National Food Safety Standard — Maximum Levels of Contaminants in Foods</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">National Food Safety Standard of the People&#39;s Republic of China</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/chinanhc2025-gb-2762-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-fda-limit-arsenic-in-rice-protein" data-anchor="does-fda-limit-arsenic-in-rice-protein" data-question="Does FDA limit arsenic in rice protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does FDA limit arsenic in rice protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

No: FDA's 100 µg/kg inorganic arsenic action level applies to infant rice cereal.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2020-ias-infant-rice-cereal-guidance|12]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2020-ias-infant-rice-cereal-guidance">Inorganic Arsenic in Rice Cereals for Infants: Action Level, Guidance for Industry</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Food and Drug Administration</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2020-ias-infant-rice-cereal-guidance">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
